<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every staircase partition, we prove that the tensor square of the corresponding irreducible complex representation of the symmetric group contains every irreducible representation of that group. This proves Saxl's conjecture.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-saxl)): For every integer $m\ge1$ and every partition $\lambda\vdash N_m$, $$g(\rho_m,\rho_m,\lambda)>0.$$ Equivalently, $S^{\rho_m}\otimes S^{\rho_m}$ contains every irreducible complex representation of $S_{N_m}$.
  - Explanation: Establishes the Saxl conjecture by proving that for every positive staircase index and every partition of the triangular integer, the Kronecker coefficient of the staircase tensor square with that partition is strictly positive.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-polytabloid)): Define $\theta_i=\#\{A\in L:\|A\|\ge i\}$, so that the block sizes are the column lengths of $\theta$. Then $\mathbb C[S_B]v_L\cong S^\theta$. The same assertion holds for any ordered basis of a dual vector space.
  - Explanation: Identifies the cyclic symmetric-group span of an alternating block tensor over a set partition with the complex Specht module whose column lengths equal the block sizes.
- **C3** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-pieri)): For $\nu\vdash a$ and $b\ge0$, $$\label{tools:pieri} \mathop{\mathrm{Ind}}_{S_a\times S_b}^{S_{a+b}} (S^\nu\boxtimes\mathbf1) \cong \bigoplus_{\substack{\lambda\vdash a+b,\ \lambda\supseteq\nu\\ \lambda/\nu\text{ a horizontal strip}}} S^\lambda.$$ Every summand occurs once.
  - Explanation: States the Pieri rule decomposing the induction of a Specht module outer-tensored with a trivial module into a multiplicity-free direct sum over horizontal strip extensions.
- **C4** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-young-rule)): Let $\theta=(\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_\ell)\vdash n$. The row-tabloid permutation module has the equivalent induced realization $$M^\theta= \mathop{\mathrm{Ind}}_{S_{\theta_1}\times\cdots\times S_{\theta_\ell}}^{S_n} \mathbf1.$$ Then $S^\tau$ occurs in $M^\theta$ if and only if $\tau\unrhd\theta$, and $S^\theta$ occurs once. The word module of any content obtained by reordering the parts of $\theta$ is also isomorphic to $M^\theta$.
  - Explanation: Characterizes the irreducible Specht constituents of the permutation module via dominance order, establishing multiplicity one for the indexing shape and invariance under reordering content parts.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-cyclic-saxl)): For every $m\ge1$ and every partition $\lambda\vdash N_m$, the irreducible $S^\lambda$ occurs in $W_m$.
  - Explanation: Strengthens the Saxl conjecture by proving that every irreducible representation of the symmetric group on the staircase triangle occurs inside the single cyclic module generated by the tensor product of row and column polytabloids.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-dominated)): If $m\ge1$, $\lambda\vdash N_m$, and $\rho_m\unrhd\lambda$, then $S^\lambda$ occurs in $W_m$.
  - Explanation: Proves that the cyclic staircase module contains every irreducible constituent whose partition is dominated by the staircase partition, using a sign twist and an equivariant surjection onto the staircase permutation module.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sector-induction)): Let a finite group $G$ act transitively on a finite set $\Omega$. Suppose a $G$-module has a direct-sum decomposition $T=\bigoplus_{A\in\Omega}T_A$ with $gT_A=T_{gA}$. Fix $D\in\Omega$, let $H$ be its stabilizer, and take $z\in T_D$. If $A_0=\mathbb C[H]z$, then the map $$\label{band:induced-map} \Phi:\mathbb C[G]\otimes_{\mathbb C[H]}A_0\longrightarrow\mathbb C[G]z, \qquad g\otimes a\longmapsto ga$$ is an isomorphism of $G$-modules.
  - Explanation: Shows that when a group permutes direct-sum coordinate sectors transitively, the cyclic module generated by a vector supported in one sector is isomorphic to the module induced from its stabilizer span.
- **C8** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-band-quotient)): For the parameters in [band:sets](#band:sets), there is a surjective $G_m$-equivariant map $$\label{band:quotient} W_m\twoheadrightarrow \mathop{\mathrm{Ind}}_{S_D\times S_F}^{S_{B_m}}(W_h\boxtimes U_{m,s}).$$ In particular, every irreducible constituent of the induced module on the right is a constituent of $W_m$.
  - Explanation: Constructs a surjective equivariant quotient from the cyclic staircase module onto the full induced module formed from a smaller cyclic staircase module and a boundary band cyclic module.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-path-contraction)): For arbitrary $X_1,\ldots,X_K\in\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}_2(\mathbb C)$, $$\label{path:contraction-formula} \left\langle u,X_1\otimes\cdots\otimes X_K\right\rangle =(-1)^{m-1} \left[X_1\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}(X_2)X_3\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}(X_4)\cdots X_{K-2}\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}(X_{K-1})X_K\right]_{11}.$$
  - Explanation: Expresses the bilinear evaluation pairing of the width-two alternating band tensor against a tensor product of two-by-two matrices as the signed top-left entry of an alternating product of matrices and adjugates.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-path-segment)): Let $e$ be the number of even positions among $p,\ldots,p+r-1$, and put $\delta=1$ if $p$ is even and $\delta=0$ otherwise. Then $$\label{path:segment-formula} A_{r,p}=r!(-1)^{e-\delta}M_1\cdots M_r.$$ In particular, all these matrices are invertible. Their values are $$\label{path:segment-table} \begin{array}{c\|cc} r & p\text{ odd} & p\text{ even}\\ \hline 1&I&I\\ 2&-2Z&2Z\\ 3&-6J&-6J\\ 4&-24I&24I \end{array}$$
  - Explanation: Evaluates the alternating matrix sum on any consecutive path segment of length at most four in the chosen Pauli-type matrix basis, showing that all summands agree and yield an invertible matrix.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-two-band)): For every $m\geq3$ and every partition $\eta\vdash 2m-1$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{len}}(\eta)\leq4$, the irreducible module $S^\eta$ occurs in $U_{m,2}$.
  - Explanation: Proves that the cyclic module generated by the width-two band vector contains every irreducible Specht module indexed by a partition of length at most four, via simultaneous matrix conjugation of the dual test basis.
- **C12** (construction, [source](#stmt-path-conjugation-example)): The change of test basis can be essential. For $m=3$ and $\eta=(2,2,1)$, the column segments have lengths $3,2$, beginning at positions $1,4$. Their product in [path:ordered-factorization](#path:ordered-factorization) is $(-6J)(2Z)=12T$, whose $(1,1)$ entry vanishes. With $$Q=\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\1&2\end{pmatrix},\qquad QTQ^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\3&-1\end{pmatrix},$$ the resulting pairing is $12$, since $(-1)^{m-1}=1$.
  - Explanation: Exhibits an explicit width-two band case where the unconjugated segment product has a vanishing top-left entry, and provides a concrete invertible matrix conjugation that makes the dual pairing nonzero.
- **C13** (classification, [source](#stmt-comb-strip-reduction)): Let $m\ge2$ and $\lambda\vdash N_m$. If $\lambda$ is not dominated by $\rho_m$, then at least one of the following holds.* 1. *There is a partition $\nu\vdash N_{m-1}$ such that $\lambda/\nu$ is a horizontal strip of size $m$.* 2. *One has $m\ge5$, and there are partitions $$\nu=\lambda^{(0)}\subset\lambda^{(1)}\subset\cdots \subset\lambda^{(q)}=\lambda, \qquad q\le4,$$ with $\nu\vdash N_{m-2}$ and every $\lambda^{(i)}/\lambda^{(i-1)}$ a nonempty horizontal strip. Their total size is $2m-1$.
  - Explanation: Establishes the combinatorial dichotomy for any partition not dominated by the staircase: either one horizontal strip of size m removes a width-one band, or at most four horizontal strips remove 2m-1 boxes to reach the staircase size two steps below.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Kronecker coefficient** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The Kronecker coefficient $$g(\alpha,\beta,\lambda) =\dim\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{S_n}(S^\lambda,S^\alpha\otimes S^\beta)$$ is the multiplicity of $S^\lambda$ in the tensor product, with $S_n$ acting diagonally.
- **Staircase partition and triangular size** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Put $$\label{intro:staircase} N_m=\frac{m(m+1)}2,\qquad \rho_m=(m,m-1,\ldots,1).$$
- **Dominance order on partitions** ([source](#sec:specht-tools)): For partitions of the same size, write $\lambda\unrhd\mu$ if $\sum_{i=1}^k\lambda_i\ge\sum_{i=1}^k\mu_i$ for every $k\ge1$.
- **Alternating block tensor** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For each block $A=(b_1,\ldots,b_r)$ choose an ordering and set $$\label{tools:block-tensor} v_A=\sum_{\pi\in S_r}\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}(\pi) \bigotimes_{i=1}^r e_{\pi(i)},\qquad v_L=\bigotimes_{A\in L}v_A,$$ where the $i$th factor of $v_A$ occupies position $b_i$.
- **Horizontal strip** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For diagrams $\nu\subseteq\lambda$, the skew diagram $\lambda/\nu$ is a *horizontal strip* if it has at most one box in each column.
- **Staircase triangle position set** ([source](#sec:dominance)): Following the triangular arrangement in [[ikenmeyer2015](#ref-ikenmeyer2015), Section 4], for $m\ge1$ use the position set $$B_m=\{(i,j)\in\mathbb Z_{\ge1}^2:i+j\le m+1\}, \qquad G_m=S_{B_m}. \label{dom:triangle}$$
- **Triangular cyclic staircase module** ([source](#sec:dominance)): With $E_m=\mathbb C^m$ and its ordered basis $e_1,\ldots,e_m$, form the alternating block tensors of [lem:polytabloid](#lem:polytabloid) and set $$w_m=v_{R_m}\otimes v_{C_m},\qquad W_m=\mathbb C[G_m]w_m \ \subseteq\ E_m^{\otimes B_m}\otimes E_m^{\otimes B_m}. \label{dom:cyclic-module}$$
- **Band generator and cyclic band module** ([source](#sec:band-quotient)): In two layers with alphabet $1,\ldots,s$, define $$\label{band:generator} u_{m,s}=v_{R_F}\otimes v_{C_F},\qquad U_{m,s}=\mathbb C[S_F]u_{m,s}.$$
- **Alternating segment matrix** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For a segment of length $r\leq4$ beginning at position $p$, define its alternating matrix by the following sum of ordered products: $$\label{path:segment-definition} A_{r,p}=\sum_{\pi\in S_r}\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}(\pi) D_p(M_{\pi(1)})D_{p+1}(M_{\pi(2)})\cdots D_{p+r-1}(M_{\pi(r)}).$$

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-205.2"
        }
      ],

      "interpretation": "Partial order comparing all prefix row sums of two partitions of equal weight.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **S1** [claim](#stmt-lem-polytabloid) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-polytabloid): Bijection from row tabloids to content words and column-stabilizer alternation sending the standard polytabloid to the alternating block tensor. -> Isomorphism of the cyclic span of v_L with the Specht module S^theta.
- **S2** [claim](#stmt-lem-young-rule) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-young-rule): Iterated application of the Pieri rule and induction on the number of parts by deleting bottom boxes from the tallest rightmost columns. -> Characterization of constituents of M^theta by dominance order with diagonal multiplicity one.
- **S3** [claim](#stmt-prop-dominated) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-dominated): Row-subgroup signed averaging, sign twist on the column factor, and equivariant pair-letter summation projecting onto the staircase permutation module. -> Occurrence of S^lambda in W_m for all partitions lambda dominated by rho_m.
- **S4** [claim](#stmt-lem-sector-induction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sector-induction): Direct-sum coordinate projection onto transitive sector translates and construction of an explicit balanced tensor inverse isomorphism. -> Isomorphism between the induced stabilizer module and the cyclic span of a sector-localized generator.
- **S5** [claim](#stmt-prop-band-quotient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-band-quotient): Local high/low alphabet pair projection forcing the high positions to be the subtriangle B_h by Ryser margin equality, followed by sector induction. -> Surjective G_m-equivariant quotient map from W_m onto Ind(W_h boxtimes U_{m,s}).
- **S6** [claim](#stmt-path-contraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-path-contraction): Index contraction along alternating row and column pairs on the width-two path using the identity J X^T J = -adj(X) at the m-1 even positions. -> Closed-form evaluation pairing as the signed (1,1) entry of an alternating matrix-adjugate product.
- **S7** [claim](#stmt-path-segment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-path-segment): Anticommutation of the traceless basis matrices Z, T, J and sign tracking under adjugation at even path indices. -> Exact invertible matrix formula A_{r,p} = r! (-1)^{e-delta} M_1 ... M_r for segment lengths 1 <= r <= 4.
- **S8** [claim](#stmt-prop-two-band) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-two-band): Factorization of the dual polytabloid pairing into invertible segment matrices and simultaneous conjugation aligning a nonzero eigenvector with the first basis vector. -> Support of every Specht module S^eta with len(eta) <= 4 inside the width-two band module U_{m,2}.
- **S9** [claim](#stmt-comb-strip-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comb-strip-reduction): Rightmost column-bottom deletion when lambda_1 >= m, and four-row prefix capacity bound combined with up to four column-bottom sweeps when lambda_1 <= m-1. -> Horizontal-strip reduction of size m to N_{m-1} or at most four horizontal strips of total size 2m-1 to N_{m-2}.
- **S10** [claim](#stmt-thm-cyclic-saxl) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comb-strip-reduction-2): Induction on m combining the dominated base case with width-one and width-two band quotients via Pieri and Littlewood-Richardson multiplicities. -> Every irreducible S^lambda for lambda of N_m occurs in the cyclic staircase module W_m.
- **S11** [claim](#stmt-thm-saxl) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comb-strip-reduction-3): Embedding the cyclic module W_m into the diagonal tensor square of the staircase Specht module S^{rho_m}. -> Strict positivity g(rho_m, rho_m, lambda) > 0 for all m >= 1 and lambda of N_m.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **INV-1**: For every integer $m\ge1$ and every partition $\lambda\vdash N_m$, the cyclic submodule $W_m$ generated by the alternating row and column staircase polytabloids inside $S^{\rho_m}\otimes S^{\rho_m}$ contains the irreducible representation $S^\lambda$, establishing $g(\rho_m,\rho_m,\lambda)>0$.
- **INV-2**: If $m\ge1$, $\lambda\vdash N_m$, and $\rho_m\unrhd\lambda$, then $S^\lambda$ occurs in $W_m$ via a sign twist and an equivariant surjection from $Z_m$ onto the permutation module $M^{\rho_m}$, where Young’s rule constructs a horizontal-strip chain and the target word $t_m$ appears with a nonzero scalar coefficient without cancellation.
- **INV-3**: For $m\geq3$ and $K=2m-1$, pairing the width-two band generator $u$ with a tensor product of two-by-two matrices contracts to the top-left entry of an alternating matrix-adjugate product; on consecutive path segments of length $r\leq4$, alternating the ordered basis of $V^*$ yields invertible segment matrices with scalar factor $r!$ times $(-1)^{e-\delta}$, and conjugating by a suitable basis change $Q\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_2(\mathbb C)$ detects every irreducible module $S^\eta$ with $\eta\vdash 2m-1$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{len}}(\eta)\leq4$ in $U_{m,2}$.
- **INV-4**: For $m\ge2$ and any partition $\lambda\vdash N_m$ not dominated by $\rho_m$, either $\lambda_1\ge m$ and deleting the bottom box of the rightmost $m$ nonempty columns leaves $\nu\vdash N_{m-1}$ with $\lambda/\nu$ a horizontal strip of size $m$, or $m\ge5$ and $q\le4$ column-bottom sweeps remove $2m-1$ boxes via nonempty horizontal strips to leave $\nu\vdash N_{m-2}$, transferring constituent support into $W_m$ through the induced band quotient.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **AU-1**: Replacing the cyclic submodule $W_m$ or the band cyclic module $U_{m,2}$ by an arbitrary vector in $S^{\rho_m}\otimes S^{\rho_m}$ or by ambient support in $S^{(m,m-1)}\otimes S^{(m,m-1)}$ during the induction. -> The coordinate projection $P_s$ maps the specific generator $w_m$ onto the factored generator in $W_h\boxtimes U_{m,s}$, so the inductive quotient requires constituents inside the cyclic modules generated by those exact projected vectors rather than merely somewhere in the ambient tensor product.
- **AU-2**: Testing whether $S^\eta$ occurs in $U_{m,2}$ by evaluating the contraction pairing of $u$ with $t_M$ only in the fixed basis $N_i=M_i$ without conjugating by $Q\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_2(\mathbb C)$. -> Although the ordered segment product $B$ is always invertible, its $(1,1)$ entry can vanish when $B$ is off-diagonal, as occurs for $m=3$ and $\eta=(2,2,1)$ where $(-6J)(2Z)=12T$ has vanishing $(1,1)$ entry.
- **AU-3**: Assuming that at most 3 horizontal strips suffice to remove $2m-1$ boxes from a non-dominated partition $\lambda\vdash N_m$ when $\lambda_1\le m-1$. -> Each horizontal strip in an eight-column diagram removes at most $8$ boxes, so for $m=13$ and $\lambda=(8^{11},3)$ of size $91=N_{13}$, 3 horizontal strips remove at most 24 boxes and cannot remove the required $25=2m-1$ boxes.
- **AU-4**: Using the cyclic quotient construction as a quantitative formula for Kronecker multiplicities, or deducing ordinary tensor-square coverage directly from asymptotic coverage, higher tensor powers, or unipotent character positivity for $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_n(q)$. -> Asymptotic coverage does not exclude exceptional partitions, cube and fourth-power results require extra tensor factors, unipotent character positivity does not imply ordinary Kronecker positivity, and the band induction retains only one copy of a suitable intermediate irreducible to certify $g(\rho_m,\rho_m,\lambda)>0$.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The proof constructs explicit Young horizontal-strip chains for dominated partitions, explicit two-by-two matrix path contractions and segment alternations for the width-two band, and explicit horizontal-strip column sweeps reducing non-dominated partitions.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object on stdin with field "mode" in {"kronecker_small", "dominance", "band_path", "strip_reduction"} and mode parameters
# PRECONDITIONS: m >= 1 integer; partitions weakly decreasing positive integers of the prescribed size; Q invertible 2x2 rational matrix
# OUTPUTS: Canonical JSON object on stdout certifying Kronecker multiplicities, Young strip chains, 2x2 path contractions, or strip reductions
# TIME: O(P(N_m)^2 * N_m) for kronecker_small (m <= 3), O(N_m) for dominance and strip_reduction, O(m) for band_path
# SPACE: O(P(N_m)^2) for kronecker_small, O(N_m) for partition chains and 2x2 flint matrices
# ARITHMETIC: Exact arbitrary-precision integer and rational arithmetic via python-flint (fmpz, fmpq, fmpq_mat)
# FAILURE: Raises ValueError and exits nonzero on invalid mode, malformed partition, non-dominated input in dominance mode, or singular Q
import sys, json
from collections import Counter
from flint import fmpz, fmpq, fmpq_mat

def conj_part(lam):
    return [sum(1 for x in lam if x >= j) for j in range(1, (lam[0] if lam else 0) + 1)]

def dominates(a, b):
    sa = sb = 0
    for k in range(max(len(a), len(b))):
        sa += a[k] if k < len(a) else 0
        sb += b[k] if k < len(b) else 0
        if sa < sb:
            return False
    return sum(a) == sum(b)

def partitions(n, max_part=None):
    if n == 0:
        yield ()
        return
    if max_part is None or max_part > n:
        max_part = n
    for first in range(max_part, 0, -1):
        for rest in partitions(n - first, first):
            yield (first,) + rest

def mn_char(lam, mu):
    if not mu:
        return 1
    r, rest = mu[0], mu[1:]
    k = len(lam)
    betas = [lam[i] + k - 1 - i for i in range(k)]
    set_b = set(betas)
    total = 0
    for i, b in enumerate(betas):
        if b >= r and (b - r) not in set_b:
            nb = sorted([b - r if idx == i else x for idx, x in enumerate(betas)], reverse=True)
            ht_minus_1 = sum(1 for x in betas if b - r < x < b)
            nlam = tuple(nb[j] - (k - 1 - j) for j in range(k) if nb[j] - (k - 1 - j) > 0)
            total += ((-1) ** ht_minus_1) * mn_char(nlam, rest)
    return total

def z_cent(mu):
    c = Counter(mu)
    res = fmpz(1)
    for k, v in c.items():
        res *= (fmpz(k) ** v) * fmpz.fac_ui(v)
    return int(res)

def solve_kronecker(m):
    if m < 1 or m > 3:
        raise ValueError("m out of small range")
    n = m * (m + 1) // 2
    rho = tuple(range(m, 0, -1))
    parts = list(partitions(n))
    n_fact = int(fmpz.fac_ui(n))
    chi_rho = {mu: mn_char(rho, mu) for mu in parts}
    out = []
    for lam in parts:
        s = sum((n_fact // z_cent(mu)) * (chi_rho[mu] ** 2) * mn_char(lam, mu) for mu in parts)
        g = s // n_fact
        branch = "dominated" if dominates(rho, lam) else ("strip_i" if lam[0] >= m else "strip_ii")
        out.append({"lambda": list(lam), "g": g, "branch": branch})
    return {"mode": "kronecker_small", "m": m, "N_m": n, "all_positive": all(x["g"] > 0 for x in out), "constituents": out}

def solve_dominance(m, lam):
    rho = list(range(m, 0, -1))
    if m < 1 or not dominates(rho, lam):
        raise ValueError("not dominated by staircase")
    cur = conj_part(lam)
    chain = [list(cur)]
    for t in reversed(rho[1:]):
        cols = conj_part(cur)
        indexed = sorted(range(len(cols)), key=lambda j: (cols[j], j), reverse=True)
        chosen = set(indexed[:t])
        new_cols = [cols[j] - (1 if j in chosen else 0) for j in range(len(cols))]
        cur = conj_part([c for c in new_cols if c > 0])
        chain.append(list(cur))
    chain.append([])
    chain.reverse()
    sign_exp = sum(r * (r - 1) // 2 for r in range(1, m + 1))
    prod_fact = fmpz(1)
    for r in range(1, m + 1):
        prod_fact *= fmpz.fac_ui(r)
    coeff = int(((-1) ** (sign_exp % 2)) * int(prod_fact))
    return {"mode": "dominance", "m": m, "dominated": True, "chain": chain, "noncancellation_coeff": coeff}

def solve_band_path(m, eta, Q):
    K = 2 * m - 1
    if m < 3 or sum(eta) != K or len(eta) > 4:
        raise ValueError("invalid band_path parameters")
    M_BASIS = [
        fmpq_mat([[1, 0], [0, 1]]),
        fmpq_mat([[1, 0], [0, -1]]),
        fmpq_mat([[0, 1], [1, 0]]),
        fmpq_mat([[0, 1], [-1, 0]]),
    ]
    cols = conj_part(eta)
    B_mat = fmpq_mat([[1, 0], [0, 1]])
    pos = 1
    for r in cols:
        e = sum(1 for q in range(pos, pos + r) if q % 2 == 0)
        delta = 1 if pos % 2 == 0 else 0
        scale = int(fmpz.fac_ui(r)) * ((-1) ** (e - delta))
        prod_M = fmpq_mat([[1, 0], [0, 1]])
        for i in range(r):
            prod_M = prod_M * M_BASIS[i]
        A_rp = prod_M * fmpq(scale)
        B_mat = B_mat * A_rp
        pos += r
    Q_mat = fmpq_mat([[fmpq(Q[0][0]), fmpq(Q[0][1])], [fmpq(Q[1][0]), fmpq(Q[1][1])]])
    if Q_mat.det() == 0:
        raise ValueError("singular Q")
    C_mat = Q_mat * B_mat * Q_mat.inv()
    sgn_m = (-1) ** (m - 1)
    p_M = str(fmpq(sgn_m) * B_mat[0, 0])
    p_N = str(fmpq(sgn_m) * C_mat[0, 0])
    return {
        "mode": "band_path",
        "m": m,
        "B": [[str(B_mat[0, 0]), str(B_mat[0, 1])], [str(B_mat[1, 0]), str(B_mat[1, 1])]],
        "det_B": str(B_mat.det()),
        "unconjugated_pairing": p_M,
        "conjugated_pairing": p_N,
    }

def solve_strip_reduction(m, lam):
    nm = m * (m + 1) // 2
    rho = list(range(m, 0, -1))
    if m < 2 or sum(lam) != nm or dominates(rho, lam):
        raise ValueError("invalid strip_reduction parameters")
    cols = conj_part(lam)
    if lam[0] >= m:
        new_cols = cols[:-m] + [c - 1 for c in cols[-m:]]
        nu = conj_part([c for c in new_cols if c > 0])
        return {"mode": "strip_reduction", "m": m, "case": "i", "s": 1, "chain": [nu, list(lam)], "strip_sizes": [m]}
    rem = 2 * m - 1
    cur = list(lam)
    seq = [list(cur)]
    sizes = []
    while rem > 0:
        c_len = cur[0] if cur else 0
        if rem > c_len:
            sizes.append(c_len)
            rem -= c_len
            cur = cur[1:]
        else:
            c_cols = conj_part(cur)
            new_cols = c_cols[:-rem] + [c - 1 for c in c_cols[-rem:]]
            cur = conj_part([c for c in new_cols if c > 0])
            sizes.append(rem)
            rem = 0
        seq.append(list(cur))
    seq.reverse()
    sizes.reverse()
    return {"mode": "strip_reduction", "m": m, "case": "ii", "s": 2, "chain": seq, "strip_sizes": sizes}

def main():
    req = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
    mode = req.get("mode")
    if mode == "kronecker_small":
        res = solve_kronecker(req["m"])
    elif mode == "dominance":
        res = solve_dominance(req["m"], req["lam"])
    elif mode == "band_path":
        res = solve_band_path(req["m"], req["eta"], req["Q"])
    elif mode == "strip_reduction":
        res = solve_strip_reduction(req["m"], req["lam"])
    else:
        raise ValueError("unknown mode")
    print(json.dumps(res, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":")))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

    "oracle_code": "import sys, json, itertools\nimport sympy as sp\n\ndef valid_part(p):\n    return isinstance(p, list) and all(isinstance(x, int) and x > 0 for x in p) and p == sorted(p, reverse=True)\n\ndef transpose_part(p):\n    if not p:\n        return []\n    return [sum(1 for r in p if r > c) for c in range(p[0])]\n\ndef is_horiz_strip(outer, inner):\n    if not valid_part(outer) or (inner != [] and not valid_part(inner)):\n        return False\n    if len(inner) > len(outer):\n        return False\n    padded_in = inner + [0] * (len(outer) - len(inner))\n    for i in range(len(outer)):\n        if not (outer[i] >= padded_in[i] >= (outer[i + 1] if i + 1 < len(outer) else 0)):\n            return False\n    return True\n\ndef dom_order(u, v):\n    if sum(u) != sum(v):\n        return False\n    for k in range(1, max(len(u), len(v)) + 1):\n        if sum(u[:k]) < sum(v[:k]):\n            return False\n    return True\n\ndef gen_parts(n, bound=None):\n    if n == 0:\n        return [()]\n    bound = n if bound is None or bound > n else bound\n    res = []\n    for h in range(bound, 0, -1):\n        for tail in gen_parts(n - h, h):\n            res.append((h,) + tail)\n    return res\n\ndef ssyt_kostka(lam, content):\n    if not content:\n        return 1 if not lam else 0\n    step = content[-1]\n    rem_c = content[:-1]\n    L = len(lam)\n    acc = 0\n    def dfs(row, left, acc_rows):\n        nonlocal acc\n        if row == L:\n            if left == 0:\n                sub = tuple(x for x in acc_rows if x > 0)\n                acc += ssyt_kostka(sub, rem_c)\n            return\n        low = lam[row + 1] if row + 1 < L else 0\n        high = lam[row]\n        for val in range(low, high + 1):\n            diff = high - val\n            if diff <= left:\n                dfs(row + 1, left - diff, acc_rows + [val])\n    dfs(0, step, [])\n    return acc\n\ndef tabloid_fixed(theta, cycle_type):\n    caps = list(theta)\n    def assign(idx):\n        if idx == len(cycle_type):\n            return 1\n        cyc = cycle_type[idx]\n        ways = 0\n        for i in range(len(caps)):\n            if caps[i] >= cyc:\n                caps[i] -= cyc\n                ways += assign(idx + 1)\n                caps[i] += cyc\n        return ways\n    return assign(0)\n\ndef perm_sign(p):\n    inv = 0\n    for i in range(len(p)):\n        for j in range(i + 1, len(p)):\n            if p[i] > p[j]:\n                inv += 1\n    return -1 if (inv % 2) else 1\n\ndef adj2(X):\n    return sp.Matrix([[X[1, 1], -X[0, 1]], [-X[1, 0], X[0, 0]]])\n\ndef verify():\n    req = json.loads(open(sys.argv[1], encoding=\"utf-8\").read())\n    raw_out = open(sys.argv[2], encoding=\"utf-8\").read().strip()\n    expected = json.loads(sys.argv[3])\n    if not raw_out:\n        sys.exit(2)\n    cand = json.loads(raw_out)\n    mode = req[\"mode\"]\n    m = req[\"m\"]\n    if mode == \"kronecker_small\":\n        n = m * (m + 1) // 2\n        pts = gen_parts(n)\n        d = len(pts)\n        K = sp.Matrix([[ssyt_kostka(pts[i], pts[j]) for j in range(d)] for i in range(d)])\n        Phi = sp.Matrix([[tabloid_fixed(pts[i], pts[j]) for j in range(d)] for i in range(d)])\n        Chi = (K.T).inv() * Phi\n        stair = tuple(range(m, 0, -1))\n        r_idx = pts.index(stair)\n        nf = int(sp.factorial(n))\n        c_sizes = []\n        for mu in pts:\n            denom = 1\n            for val in set(mu):\n                cnt = mu.count(val)\n                denom *= (val ** cnt) * int(sp.factorial(cnt))\n            c_sizes.append(nf // denom)\n        rows = cand.get(\"constituents\", [])\n        if len(rows) != d or not cand.get(\"all_positive\"):\n            sys.exit(4)\n        for idx, pt in enumerate(pts):\n            g_val = sum(c_sizes[j] * (int(Chi[r_idx, j]) ** 2) * int(Chi[idx, j]) for j in range(d)) // nf\n            if g_val <= 0 or rows[idx][\"lambda\"] != list(pt) or rows[idx][\"g\"] != g_val:\n                sys.exit(5)\n    elif mode == \"dominance\":\n        lam = req[\"lam\"]\n        stair = list(range(m, 0, -1))\n        if not dom_order(stair, lam):\n            sys.exit(6)\n        tau = transpose_part(lam)\n        ch = cand[\"chain\"]\n        if len(ch) != m + 1 or ch[0] != [] or ch[-1] != tau:\n            sys.exit(7)\n        for k in range(1, m + 1):\n            prev_p, cur_p = ch[k - 1], ch[k]\n            if not is_horiz_strip(cur_p, prev_p):\n                sys.exit(8)\n            if sum(cur_p) - sum(prev_p) != stair[k - 1]:\n                sys.exit(9)\n            if not dom_order(cur_p, stair[:k]):\n                sys.exit(10)\n        exp_sgn = sum(int(sp.binomial(r, 2)) for r in range(1, m + 1))\n        exp_prd = 1\n        for r in range(1, m + 1):\n            exp_prd *= int(sp.factorial(r))\n        if cand[\"noncancellation_coeff\"] != ((-1) ** exp_sgn) * exp_prd or cand[\"noncancellation_coeff\"] == 0:\n            sys.exit(11)\n    elif mode == \"band_path\":\n        eta = req[\"eta\"]\n        Q_in = req[\"Q\"]\n        if m < 3 or sum(eta) != 2 * m - 1 or len(eta) > 4:\n            sys.exit(12)\n        M_list = [\n            sp.Matrix([[1, 0], [0, 1]]),\n            sp.Matrix([[1, 0], [0, -1]]),\n            sp.Matrix([[0, 1], [1, 0]]),\n            sp.Matrix([[0, 1], [-1, 0]]),\n        ]\n        Q_sp = sp.Matrix([[sp.Rational(Q_in[0][0]), sp.Rational(Q_in[0][1])],\n                          [sp.Rational(Q_in[1][0]), sp.Rational(Q_in[1][1])]])\n        Q_inv = Q_sp.inv()\n        N_list = [Q_sp * X * Q_inv for X in M_list]\n        col_lens = transpose_part(eta)\n        p_idx = 1\n        prod_M_seg = sp.eye(2)\n        prod_N_seg = sp.eye(2)\n        for r_len in col_lens:\n            seg_M = sp.zeros(2, 2)\n            seg_N = sp.zeros(2, 2)\n            for perm in itertools.permutations(range(r_len)):\n                sgn = perm_sign(perm)\n                term_M = sp.eye(2)\n                term_N = sp.eye(2)\n                for offset, p_val in enumerate(perm):\n                    q_pos = p_idx + offset\n                    mat_m = M_list[p_val]\n                    mat_n = N_list[p_val]\n                    if q_pos % 2 == 0:\n                        mat_m = adj2(mat_m)\n                        mat_n = adj2(mat_n)\n                    term_M = term_M * mat_m\n                    term_N = term_N * mat_n\n                seg_M += sgn * term_M\n                seg_N += sgn * term_N\n            prod_M_seg = prod_M_seg * seg_M\n            prod_N_seg = prod_N_seg * seg_N\n            p_idx += r_len\n        if prod_M_seg.det() == 0 or str(prod_M_seg.det()) != cand[\"det_B\"]:\n            sys.exit(13)\n        sgn_m = (-1) ** (m - 1)\n        if str(sgn_m * prod_M_seg[0, 0]) != cand[\"unconjugated_pairing\"]:\n            sys.exit(14)\n        if str(sgn_m * prod_N_seg[0, 0]) != cand[\"conjugated_pairing\"] or prod_N_seg[0, 0] == 0:\n            sys.exit(15)\n    elif mode == \"strip_reduction\":\n        lam = req[\"lam\"]\n        stair = list(range(m, 0, -1))\n        if m < 2 or sum(lam) != m * (m + 1) // 2 or dom_order(stair, lam):\n            sys.exit(16)\n        ch = cand[\"chain\"]\n        sz = cand[\"strip_sizes\"]\n        if ch[-1] != lam or len(ch) != len(sz) + 1:\n            sys.exit(17)\n        if lam[0] >= m:\n            if cand[\"case\"] != \"i\" or cand[\"s\"] != 1 or len(sz) != 1 or sz[0] != m:\n                sys.exit(18)\n            target_rem = (m - 1) * m // 2\n        else:\n            if m < 5 or cand[\"case\"] != \"ii\" or cand[\"s\"] != 2 or not (1 <= len(sz) <= 4) or sum(sz) != 2 * m - 1:\n                sys.exit(19)\n            target_rem = (m - 2) * (m - 1) // 2\n        if sum(ch[0]) != target_rem or not valid_part(ch[0]):\n            sys.exit(20)\n        for k in range(len(sz)):\n            if not is_horiz_strip(ch[k + 1], ch[k]):\n                sys.exit(21)\n            if sum(ch[k + 1]) - sum(ch[k]) != sz[k] or sz[k] <= 0:\n                sys.exit(22)\n    else:\n        sys.exit(23)\n    if cand != expected:\n        sys.exit(3)\n    sys.exit(0)\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    verify()\n",
    "output_contract": "Canonical JSON object certifying exact Kronecker multiplicities $g(\\rho_m,\\rho_m,\\lambda)>0$, Young horizontal-strip chains and noncancellation coefficients, $2\\times2$ matrix path contractions and conjugated pairings, or horizontal-strip sweep reductions of size $m$ or $2m-1$ in $q\\le4$ strips.",
    "reason": "The proof constructs explicit Young horizontal-strip chains for dominated partitions, explicit two-by-two matrix path contractions and segment alternations for the width-two band, and explicit horizontal-strip column sweeps reducing non-dominated partitions.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON object on stdin with field \"mode\" in {\"kronecker_small\", \"dominance\", \"band_path\", \"strip_reduction\"} and mode parameters\n# PRECONDITIONS: m >= 1 integer; partitions weakly decreasing positive integers of the prescribed size; Q invertible 2x2 rational matrix\n# OUTPUTS: Canonical JSON object on stdout certifying Kronecker multiplicities, Young strip chains, 2x2 path contractions, or strip reductions\n# TIME: O(P(N_m)^2 * N_m) for kronecker_small (m <= 3), O(N_m) for dominance and strip_reduction, O(m) for band_path\n# SPACE: O(P(N_m)^2) for kronecker_small, O(N_m) for partition chains and 2x2 flint matrices\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact arbitrary-precision integer and rational arithmetic via python-flint (fmpz, fmpq, fmpq_mat)\n# FAILURE: Raises ValueError and exits nonzero on invalid mode, malformed partition, non-dominated input in dominance mode, or singular Q\nimport sys, json\nfrom collections import Counter\nfrom flint import fmpz, fmpq, fmpq_mat\n\ndef conj_part(lam):\n    return [sum(1 for x in lam if x >= j) for j in range(1, (lam[0] if lam else 0) + 1)]\n\ndef dominates(a, b):\n    sa = sb = 0\n    for k in range(max(len(a), len(b))):\n        sa += a[k] if k < len(a) else 0\n        sb += b[k] if k < len(b) else 0\n        if sa < sb:\n            return False\n    return sum(a) == sum(b)\n\ndef partitions(n, max_part=None):\n    if n == 0:\n        yield ()\n        return\n    if max_part is None or max_part > n:\n        max_part = n\n    for first in range(max_part, 0, -1):\n        for rest in partitions(n - first, first):\n            yield (first,) + rest\n\ndef mn_char(lam, mu):\n    if not mu:\n        return 1\n    r, rest = mu[0], mu[1:]\n    k = len(lam)\n    betas = [lam[i] + k - 1 - i for i in range(k)]\n    set_b = set(betas)\n    total = 0\n    for i, b in enumerate(betas):\n        if b >= r and (b - r) not in set_b:\n            nb = sorted([b - r if idx == i else x for idx, x in enumerate(betas)], reverse=True)\n            ht_minus_1 = sum(1 for x in betas if b - r < x < b)\n            nlam = tuple(nb[j] - (k - 1 - j) for j in range(k) if nb[j] - (k - 1 - j) > 0)\n            total += ((-1) ** ht_minus_1) * mn_char(nlam, rest)\n    return total\n\ndef z_cent(mu):\n    c = Counter(mu)\n    res = fmpz(1)\n    for k, v in c.items():\n        res *= (fmpz(k) ** v) * fmpz.fac_ui(v)\n    return int(res)\n\ndef solve_kronecker(m):\n    if m < 1 or m > 3:\n        raise ValueError(\"m out of small range\")\n    n = m * (m + 1) // 2\n    rho = tuple(range(m, 0, -1))\n    parts = list(partitions(n))\n    n_fact = int(fmpz.fac_ui(n))\n    chi_rho = {mu: mn_char(rho, mu) for mu in parts}\n    out = []\n    for lam in parts:\n        s = sum((n_fact // z_cent(mu)) * (chi_rho[mu] ** 2) * mn_char(lam, mu) for mu in parts)\n        g = s // n_fact\n        branch = \"dominated\" if dominates(rho, lam) else (\"strip_i\" if lam[0] >= m else \"strip_ii\")\n        out.append({\"lambda\": list(lam), \"g\": g, \"branch\": branch})\n    return {\"mode\": \"kronecker_small\", \"m\": m, \"N_m\": n, \"all_positive\": all(x[\"g\"] > 0 for x in out), \"constituents\": out}\n\ndef solve_dominance(m, lam):\n    rho = list(range(m, 0, -1))\n    if m < 1 or not dominates(rho, lam):\n        raise ValueError(\"not dominated by staircase\")\n    cur = conj_part(lam)\n    chain = [list(cur)]\n    for t in reversed(rho[1:]):\n        cols = conj_part(cur)\n        indexed = sorted(range(len(cols)), key=lambda j: (cols[j], j), reverse=True)\n        chosen = set(indexed[:t])\n        new_cols = [cols[j] - (1 if j in chosen else 0) for j in range(len(cols))]\n        cur = conj_part([c for c in new_cols if c > 0])\n        chain.append(list(cur))\n    chain.append([])\n    chain.reverse()\n    sign_exp = sum(r * (r - 1) // 2 for r in range(1, m + 1))\n    prod_fact = fmpz(1)\n    for r in range(1, m + 1):\n        prod_fact *= fmpz.fac_ui(r)\n    coeff = int(((-1) ** (sign_exp % 2)) * int(prod_fact))\n    return {\"mode\": \"dominance\", \"m\": m, \"dominated\": True, \"chain\": chain, \"noncancellation_coeff\": coeff}\n\ndef solve_band_path(m, eta, Q):\n    K = 2 * m - 1\n    if m < 3 or sum(eta) != K or len(eta) > 4:\n        raise ValueError(\"invalid band_path parameters\")\n    M_BASIS = [\n        fmpq_mat([[1, 0], [0, 1]]),\n        fmpq_mat([[1, 0], [0, -1]]),\n        fmpq_mat([[0, 1], [1, 0]]),\n        fmpq_mat([[0, 1], [-1, 0]]),\n    ]\n    cols = conj_part(eta)\n    B_mat = fmpq_mat([[1, 0], [0, 1]])\n    pos = 1\n    for r in cols:\n        e = sum(1 for q in range(pos, pos + r) if q % 2 == 0)\n        delta = 1 if pos % 2 == 0 else 0\n        scale = int(fmpz.fac_ui(r)) * ((-1) ** (e - delta))\n        prod_M = fmpq_mat([[1, 0], [0, 1]])\n        for i in range(r):\n            prod_M = prod_M * M_BASIS[i]\n        A_rp = prod_M * fmpq(scale)\n        B_mat = B_mat * A_rp\n        pos += r\n    Q_mat = fmpq_mat([[fmpq(Q[0][0]), fmpq(Q[0][1])], [fmpq(Q[1][0]), fmpq(Q[1][1])]])\n    if Q_mat.det() == 0:\n        raise ValueError(\"singular Q\")\n    C_mat = Q_mat * B_mat * Q_mat.inv()\n    sgn_m = (-1) ** (m - 1)\n    p_M = str(fmpq(sgn_m) * B_mat[0, 0])\n    p_N = str(fmpq(sgn_m) * C_mat[0, 0])\n    return {\n        \"mode\": \"band_path\",\n        \"m\": m,\n        \"B\": [[str(B_mat[0, 0]), str(B_mat[0, 1])], [str(B_mat[1, 0]), str(B_mat[1, 1])]],\n        \"det_B\": str(B_mat.det()),\n        \"unconjugated_pairing\": p_M,\n        \"conjugated_pairing\": p_N,\n    }\n\ndef solve_strip_reduction(m, lam):\n    nm = m * (m + 1) // 2\n    rho = list(range(m, 0, -1))\n    if m < 2 or sum(lam) != nm or dominates(rho, lam):\n        raise ValueError(\"invalid strip_reduction parameters\")\n    cols = conj_part(lam)\n    if lam[0] >= m:\n        new_cols = cols[:-m] + [c - 1 for c in cols[-m:]]\n        nu = conj_part([c for c in new_cols if c > 0])\n        return {\"mode\": \"strip_reduction\", \"m\": m, \"case\": \"i\", \"s\": 1, \"chain\": [nu, list(lam)], \"strip_sizes\": [m]}\n    rem = 2 * m - 1\n    cur = list(lam)\n    seq = [list(cur)]\n    sizes = []\n    while rem > 0:\n        c_len = cur[0] if cur else 0\n        if rem > c_len:\n            sizes.append(c_len)\n            rem -= c_len\n            cur = cur[1:]\n        else:\n            c_cols = conj_part(cur)\n            new_cols = c_cols[:-rem] + [c - 1 for c in c_cols[-rem:]]\n            cur = conj_part([c for c in new_cols if c > 0])\n            sizes.append(rem)\n            rem = 0\n        seq.append(list(cur))\n    seq.reverse()\n    sizes.reverse()\n    return {\"mode\": \"strip_reduction\", \"m\": m, \"case\": \"ii\", \"s\": 2, \"chain\": seq, \"strip_sizes\": sizes}\n\ndef main():\n    req = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())\n    mode = req.get(\"mode\")\n    if mode == \"kronecker_small\":\n        res = solve_kronecker(req[\"m\"])\n    elif mode == \"dominance\":\n        res = solve_dominance(req[\"m\"], req[\"lam\"])\n    elif mode == \"band_path\":\n        res = solve_band_path(req[\"m\"], req[\"eta\"], req[\"Q\"])\n    elif mode == \"strip_reduction\":\n        res = solve_strip_reduction(req[\"m\"], req[\"lam\"])\n    else:\n        raise ValueError(\"unknown mode\")\n    print(json.dumps(res, sort_keys=True, separators=(\",\", \":\")))\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n",
    "test_vectors": [
      {
        "expected": "{\"N_m\":1,\"all_positive\":true,\"constituents\":[{\"branch\":\"dominated\",\"g\":1,\"lambda\":[1]}],\"m\":1,\"mode\":\"kronecker_small\"}",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | Symbolic algebra and representation-theory software packages can certify Kronecker positivity $g(\rho_m,\rho_m,\lambda)>0$ for staircase partitions $\rho_m$ and construct recursive constituent reduction chains inside the cyclic module $W_m$ using the dominance check, horizontal-strip reduction, and $2\times2$ matrix path contraction. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves Kronecker positivity for complex representations of symmetric groups and introduces no game-theoretic models, dynamical simulations, or stochastic state-transition systems. |
| tools | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | Combinatorial and algebraic verification tools for symmetric group representations can implement the closed-form $2\times2$ segment alternation table and basis conjugation to construct explicit dual-polytabloid witnesses for constituents of the width-two band module $U_{m,2}$. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The paper gives explicit, finite, deterministic procedures for horizontal-strip reduction of non-dominated partitions (Lemma 13), dominance-preserving horizontal-strip extraction for Young's rule (Lemma 4), and $2\times2$ matrix-adjugate path contraction with segment alternation (Lemmas 9 and 10, Proposition 11). |
| data_structures | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The proofs rely on explicit combinatorial indexing layouts: the triangular coordinate grid $B_m$ with orthogonal row and column set partitions $R_m$ and $C_m$, its decomposition into a smaller triangle $B_{m-s}$ and boundary band $F$, and the linear zigzag path indexing that turns two-layer alternating block tensors into a one-dimensional $2\times2$ matrix contraction. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper operates purely in algebraic representation theory over $\mathbb C$ and specifies no hardware circuits, accelerator pipelines, memory hierarchies, or physical execution targets. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper establishes equivariant linear maps and Kronecker coefficient positivity for symmetric group representations and contains no cryptographic primitives, hardness assumptions, or security models. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper studies exact algebraic decompositions of symmetric group modules over $\mathbb C$ and introduces no statistical learning objectives, gradient-based optimization, or data-driven inference models. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Although the proofs use elementary extremal identities (a sum-of-squares minimum on row sums and the unique equality case of Ryser's zero-one matrix margin inequalities), the paper formulates no operations-research or mathematical-programming optimization methods. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The coordinate sectors are direct summands of a finite-dimensional complex vector space used to realize group representation induction, with no connection to distributed systems or networked execution. |
| other (representation_theory_and_algebraic_combinatorics) | True | strong | implementable | stated | The paper directly belongs to the representation theory of symmetric groups and algebraic combinatorics, proving Saxl's conjecture ($g(\rho_m,\rho_m,\lambda)>0$ for all $\lambda\vdash N_m$) and its cyclic strengthening ($S^\lambda$ in $W_m$) through explicit polytabloid constructions, coordinate-sector induction, $2\times2$ matrix path contractions, and horizontal-strip reductions. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **LIM-1** (formal_scope): The proof establishes strict positivity $g(\rho_m,\rho_m,\lambda)>0$ for every partition $\lambda\vdash N_m$ and every integer $m\ge1$, addressing constituent support rather than providing an explicit formula for Kronecker multiplicities. Blocked use: ['Computing exact numerical values or closed-form expressions for the Kronecker multiplicities beyond strict positivity $g(\\rho_m,\\rho_m,\\lambda)>0$.', 'Extrapolating constituent multiplicity formulas from the single-copy inductive reduction.']
- **LIM-2** (hypothesis): All representations and tensor products are restricted to finite-dimensional complex modules over $\mathbb C$, relying on complete reducibility so that any irreducible quotient of $W_m$ is also a submodule constituent. Blocked use: ['Applying the quotient-to-submodule constituent transfer directly to modular representations over fields of positive characteristic where semisimplicity fails.', 'Assuming the unnormalized signed diagonal average $u$ remains nonzero over fields whose characteristic divides $\|H_R\|=\\prod_{r=1}^m r!$.']
- **LIM-3** (hypothesis): The direct theorem covers symmetric groups $S_{N_m}$ of triangular degree $N_m$ indexed by the staircase partition $\rho_m$ with parts $m,m-1,\ldots,1$; general degrees $n\notin\{2,4,9\}$ require the companion construction outside this paper. Blocked use: ['Directly invoking the staircase tensor square $S^{\\rho_m}\\otimes S^{\\rho_m}$ for symmetric groups $S_n$ when $n$ is not a triangular number $N_m$.', 'Claiming universal irreducible tensor squares exist for $S_n$ outside the range $n\\notin\\{2,4,9\\}$.']
- **LIM-4** (hypothesis): The dominance proposition guarantees constituent support in $W_m$ only for partitions $\lambda\vdash N_m$ dominated by the staircase ($\rho_m\unrhd\lambda$); shapes outside this dominance range require band-quotient induction and horizontal-strip reductions. Blocked use: ['Using the permutation-module surjection onto $M^{\\rho_m}$ alone to detect constituents $S^\\lambda$ when $\\lambda$ is not dominated by $\\rho_m$.']
- **LIM-5** (hypothesis): The width-two band cyclic module $U_{m,2}$ is analysed for $m\geq3$ and detects irreducible modules $S^\eta$ for partitions $\eta\vdash 2m-1$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{len}}(\eta)\leq4$, because the dual-polytabloid segment alternation uses the four-element basis of $\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}_2(\mathbb C)$. Blocked use: ['Applying the width-two band segment alternation lemma to column segments beyond $r\\leq4$, where alternating sums over a four-dimensional space $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Mat}}_2(\\mathbb C)$ vanish.', 'Invoking the width-two band support proposition outside the regime $m\\geq3$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{len}}(\\eta)\\leq4$.']
- **LIM-6** (edge_case): In the width-two band path contraction, the ordered product $B$ of alternating segment matrices is invertible in the un-conjugated basis $N_i=M_i$, but its $(1,1)$ entry can vanish, requiring a simultaneous basis conjugation $N_i=QM_iQ^{-1}$ by $Q\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_2(\mathbb C)$ to make the endpoint pairing nonzero. Blocked use: ['Testing dual-polytabloid nonvanishing in $U_{m,2}$ using the fixed basis $N_i=M_i$ without basis conjugation, as demonstrated by $m=3$ and $\\eta=(2,2,1)$ where $(-6J)(2Z)=12T$ has vanishing $(1,1)$ entry.']
- **LIM-7** (counterexample_scope): In the multi-strip combinatorial reduction for non-dominated partitions with $\lambda_1\le m-1$, one has $m\ge5$ and up to $q\le4$ nonempty horizontal strips of total size $2m-1$ are required; fewer than four horizontal strips do not suffice in general, as shown at $m=13$ by $\lambda=(8^{11},3)$ of size $91=N_{13}$. Blocked use: ['Restricting the multi-strip horizontal reduction to three or fewer horizontal strips when $\\lambda_1\\le m-1$, which fails for $m=13$ and $\\lambda=(8^{11},3)$ because three strips in its eight-column diagram cannot remove the required $25=2m-1$ boxes.']
- **LIM-8** (nonconstructive): In the multi-strip inductive step, the intermediate band partition $\eta\vdash2m-1$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{len}}(\eta)\leq4$ is obtained existentially from $a_\eta>0$ and $c_{\nu,\eta}^{\lambda}>0$, and ambient tensor-product support alone is insufficient without tracking the cyclic modules $W_{m-s}$ and $U_{m,s}$. Blocked use: ['Replacing the cyclic module $W_m$ or the projected band module $U_{m,2}$ by arbitrary vectors in the ambient tensor squares during the inductive step.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 205 (`lean/docs/205.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Universal Tensor Squares for Symmetric Groups](../../preprints/Universal-Tensor-Squares-for-Symmetric-Groups-September-24-2026/main.pdf)
> - [A Cyclic Polytabloid Proof of Saxl's Conjecture](../../preprints/A-Cyclic-Polytabloid-Proof-of-Saxls-Conjecture-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves the universal tensor-square conjecture for symmetric groups in the stated range. For every positive integer $n\notin\{2,4,9\}$, it constructs an irreducible complex representation of $S_n$ whose tensor square contains every irreducible complex representation of $S_n$. Equivalently, all corresponding Kronecker coefficients are positive. The statement also supplies injective intertwining maps for arbitrary finite-dimensional irreducible representations.
>
> Saxl's conjecture asserts that the tensor square of each staircase Specht module contains every irreducible representation of the corresponding symmetric group. The formalization establishes this for every $m\ge1$: if $\rho_m=(m,m-1,\ldots,1)$, then $g(\rho_m,\rho_m,\mu)>0$ for every partition $\mu$ of $m(m+1)/2$.
>
> The stronger claim that every constituent appears in the orbit span of one prescribed tensor is not included.

Comparator source `Saxl.lean` (4450 bytes, sha256 `71917234c1dc2e7fe3b8912175f5bdbb30565803ff71a1bb5ce71c889c79d683`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

open scoped TensorProduct

universe uG uV

namespace Saxl

/- Coefficients of position tensors on an alphabet of size `d`. -/
abbrev WordSpace (n d : ℕ) := (Fin n → Fin d) → ℂ

/- The position action satisfies `g e_a = e_(a ∘ g⁻¹)`. -/
def wordRep (n d : ℕ) : Representation ℂ (Equiv.Perm (Fin n)) (WordSpace n d) where
  toFun g :=
    { toFun := fun f a => f (a ∘ g)
      map_add' := by intros; rfl
      map_smul' := by intros; rfl }
  map_one' := by ext f a; rfl
  map_mul' g h := by ext f a; rfl

/- The cyclic subrepresentation generated by an actual vector. -/
def cyclic {G : Type uG} {V : Type uV} [Group G] [AddCommGroup V] [Module ℂ V]
    (ρ : Representation ℂ G V) (v : V) : Subrepresentation ρ where
  toSubmodule := Submodule.span ℂ (Set.range fun g : G => ρ g v)
  apply_mem_toSubmodule g := by
    intro x hx
    induction hx using Submodule.span_induction with
    | mem x hx =>
      obtain ⟨h, rfl⟩ := hx
      apply Submodule.subset_span
      exact ⟨g * h, by simp only [map_mul, Module.End.mul_apply]⟩
    | zero => simp
    | add x y hx hy ihx ihy =>
      simpa only [map_add] using Submodule.add_mem _ ihx ihy
    | smul a x hx ih =>
      simpa only [map_smul] using Submodule.smul_mem _ a ih

/- A tableau is an enumeration of all cells by the position set. -/
abbrev Tableau (n : ℕ) (μ : YoungDiagram) := Fin n ≃ μ.cells

/- One fixed tableau; changing its enumeration gives an isomorphic Specht module. -/
def canonicalTableau {n : ℕ} (μ : YoungDiagram) (hn : μ.card = n) : Tableau n μ :=
  (Fintype.equivFinOfCardEq (by simpa using hn)).symm

/- The row word corresponding to the tabloid of a tableau. -/
def rowWord {n : ℕ} {μ : YoungDiagram} (t : Tableau n μ) : Fin n → Fin (μ.colLen 0) :=
  fun i => ⟨(t i).val.1, YoungDiagram.mem_iff_lt_colLen.mp
    (μ.up_left_mem le_rfl (Nat.zero_le _) (t i).property)⟩

/- The column permutations of a tableau. -/
def columnGroup {n : ℕ} {μ : YoungDiagram} (t : Tableau n μ) :
    Subgroup (Equiv.Perm (Fin n)) where
  carrier := {g | ∀ i, (t (g i)).val.2 = (t i).val.2}
  one_mem' := by intro i; rfl
  mul_mem' := by
    intro g h hg hh i
    exact (hg (h i)).trans (hh i)
  inv_mem' := by
    intro g hg i
    have h := hg (g⁻¹ i)
    simpa using h.symm

/- The standard column-antisymmetrized row tabloid, with complex coefficients. -/
def polytabloid {n : ℕ} {μ : YoungDiagram} (t : Tableau n μ) :
    WordSpace n (μ.colLen 0) := by
  classical
  letI := Fintype.ofFinite (columnGroup t)
  exact ∑ g : columnGroup t,
    (((Equiv.Perm.sign (g : Equiv.Perm (Fin n))) : ℤ) : ℂ) •
      wordRep n (μ.colLen 0) (g : Equiv.Perm (Fin n)) (Pi.single (rowWord t) 1)

/- The Specht module, defined as the orbit span of a polytabloid in the
row-tabloid permutation module, realized inside the word space. -/
def spechtSub {n : ℕ} {μ : YoungDiagram} (t : Tableau n μ) :=
  cyclic (wordRep n (μ.colLen 0)) (polytabloid t)

abbrev Specht {n : ℕ} {μ : YoungDiagram} (t : Tableau n μ) :=
  (spechtSub t).toSubmodule

/- The genuine symmetric-group representation on the complex Specht module. -/
def spechtRep {n : ℕ} {μ : YoungDiagram} (t : Tableau n μ) :
    Representation ℂ (Equiv.Perm (Fin n)) (Specht t) :=
  (spechtSub t).toRepresentation

/- Kronecker multiplicity is the complex dimension of Hom, with the
diagonal symmetric-group action on the tensor product. -/
def kronecker {n : ℕ} {α β μ : YoungDiagram}
    (a : Tableau n α) (b : Tableau n β) (t : Tableau n μ) : ℕ :=
  Module.finrank ℂ (Representation.IntertwiningMap (spechtRep t)
    ((spechtRep a).tprod (spechtRep b)))


def staircase (m : ℕ) : YoungDiagram where
  cells := ((Finset.range m) ×ˢ (Finset.range m)).filter (fun x => x.1 + x.2 < m)
  isLowerSet := by
    intro x y hxy hy
    simp only [Finset.mem_coe, Finset.mem_filter, Finset.mem_product,
      Finset.mem_range] at hy ⊢
    exact ⟨⟨lt_of_le_of_lt hxy.1 hy.1.1, lt_of_le_of_lt hxy.2 hy.1.2⟩,
      lt_of_le_of_lt (Nat.add_le_add hxy.1 hxy.2) hy.2⟩


def SaxlConjecture : Prop :=
  ∀ (m : ℕ), 1 ≤ m → ∀ (μ : YoungDiagram) (hμ : μ.card = (staircase m).card),
    0 < kronecker (canonicalTableau (staircase m) rfl)
      (canonicalTableau (staircase m) rfl) (canonicalTableau μ hμ)

theorem saxl_conjecture : SaxlConjecture := by
  sorry

end Saxl

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every staircase partition, we prove that the tensor square of the corresponding irreducible complex representation of the symmetric group contains every irreducible representation of that group. This proves Saxl's conjecture.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
